<link rel="stylesheet" href="/pro-log/assets/alopex/katex.min-b4834341aeb6.css"><link rel="stylesheet" href="/pro-log/assets/alopex/article-9f0f7a524f10.css">

###### Constrain the claim before scaling again

<section id="prologue" class="paper-section" data-section="01" aria-label="Constrain the claim before scaling again"><aside class="in-brief" aria-label="Section summary"><span>In brief</span><div><p>This article reconstructs the ALOPEX neural-training program from source code, frozen experiment records, verified arithmetic, and external literature. The sequence of experiments changed four components of the learning system: <strong>measurement</strong>, <strong>credit space</strong>, <strong>persistent state</strong>, and <strong>actuation</strong>. The recorded results establish parameter-scale feasibility for some variants and bounded neural-network learning for Learned Credit-Map Hybrid. They do not establish ALOPEX as a competitive general-purpose alternative to gradient-based training.</p></div></aside><p>The article uses chronological reconstruction only where source records establish the sequence. It does not assign undocumented intent to missing experiments or reinterpret unrecovered versions as successful or failed ablations.</p><p>The documented sequence is: global consequence measurements; temporal reuse of consequence; same-batch replay; block-local interventions; hidden activation interventions; exact directional measurements; and finally a learned output-to-hidden credit operator. Each transition changed the information available to the learner or the representation in which that information was stored.</p><aside id="claim-prologue-close" class="claim claim-derived" data-claim-kind="derived"><header><span>Derived / analytical conclusion</span><code>claim-prologue-close</code></header><div class="claim-body"><p>The experiments support treating <strong>measurement, credit space, persistent state, and actuation as separate algorithmic components</strong>. Changes to one component did not by themselves determine the behavior of the complete optimizer.</p></div></aside><p>The historical ALOPEX lineage itself is older than neural-network optimization. Harth and Tzanakou used adaptive perturbation and correlation to identify visual receptive fields. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-harth1974alopex" id="citation-harth1974alopex-1">Harth &amp; Tzanakou, 1974</a></span> Unnikrishnan and Venugopal later formulated ALOPEX for feedforward and recurrent neural networks, assigning learning signal from correlations between parameter changes and changes in global error. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-unnikrishnan1994alopex" id="citation-unnikrishnan1994alopex-1">Unnikrishnan &amp; Venugopal, 1994</a></span> Bia simplified that neural ALOPEX family further. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-bia2001alopexb" id="citation-bia2001alopexb-1">Bia, 2001</a></span></p><p>Those papers establish the historical family around repeated perturbation, observed consequence, and retained correlation. Later variants in this repository alter the state representation, measurement channel, and actuator and are therefore described by their explicit mechanisms rather than by name alone.</p><p>The primary historical baseline is now available rather than inferred from recovered code alone. Roy's 2023 MSc thesis reports that mini-batch ALOPEX-B on a 784→10 MNIST logistic model improved over SGD but did not converge as quickly as Adam, while a gradient-seeded direction variant reported lower training loss in fewer epochs than both Adam and SGD in that small-model experiment. The thesis also identifies the one-global-error/high-dimensional search problem explicitly and proposes fused GPU execution and momentum as future work. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-roy2023thesis" id="citation-roy2023thesis-1">Roy, 2023</a></span> These results are important historical evidence, but they are not evidence for hidden-layer credit assignment or SLM-scale training.</p><p>The general neural-training claim is evaluated below using the repository's own quality, depth, scale, and timing results together with external methods that address the same credit-assignment problem.</p><p>The five research threads are connected, but they answer different questions. Threads I–III are retrospective and evidence-led. Thread IV separates the strong general-competitiveness claim from the narrower hypotheses that remain unresolved. Thread V is prospective: it asks for the missing SLM-scale test rather than redirecting the programme to a different optimization problem.</p><div class="evidence-legend" aria-label="Claim evidence legend"><span class="legend-literature"><i></i>Literature / external evidence</span><span class="legend-frozen"><i></i>Frozen empirical evidence</span><span class="legend-derived"><i></i>Derived / analytical conclusion</span><span class="legend-hypothesis"><i></i>Hypothesis / future test</span><span class="legend-limitation"><i></i>Limitation / evidence boundary</span></div><h3>Research questions</h3><p>This retrospective is organized around four empirical questions rather than one optimizer leaderboard:</p><ol>
<li>What information does each intervention actually observe?</li>
<li>Does making a directional coefficient exact improve learning when credit space, memory, and actuator are held fixed?</li>
<li>What changes when the persistent object becomes a learned credit map rather than a correlation trace?</li>
<li>How do quality, depth, parameter scale, and systems cost change together—and what evidence would actually close the neural-training hypothesis?</li>
</ol><p>The article therefore keeps <strong>mechanism experiments</strong>, <strong>full-quality comparisons</strong>, <strong>systems measurements</strong>, <strong>historical audits</strong>, and <strong>teaching models</strong> as distinct evidence classes. A positive result in one class does not establish another.</p><h3>Literature review: alternatives to conventional backpropagation</h3><p>The relevant literature is heterogeneous because “without backpropagation” can refer to different information constraints. The methods below are grouped by the information they use and by the credit-assignment problem they solve.</p><p><strong>Function-value and zeroth-order optimization.</strong> SPSA estimates update directions from paired objective evaluations without coordinatewise derivatives. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-spall1992spsa" id="citation-spall1992spsa-1">Spall, 1992</a></span> Modern random-direction and derivative-free analyses make the dependence on dimension, structure, and oracle assumptions explicit. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-nesterov2017random" id="citation-nesterov2017random-1">Nesterov &amp; Spokoiny, 2017</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-larson2019dfo" id="citation-larson2019dfo-1">Larson et al., 2019</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-liu2020primer" id="citation-liu2020primer-1">Liu et al., 2020</a></span> MeZO demonstrates an in-place zeroth-order implementation for pretrained language-model fine-tuning, including nondifferentiable task objectives, while DeepZero adds coordinate structure, sparsity, and feature reuse for from-scratch deep-network training. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-malladi2023mezo" id="citation-malladi2023mezo-1">Malladi et al., 2023</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-chen2024deepzero" id="citation-chen2024deepzero-1">Chen et al., 2024</a></span> These methods establish that forward-only or function-value optimization can be practical in selected settings; their access assumptions and training regimes differ from ALOPEX's feed-forward CIFAR experiments.</p><p><strong>Forward-mode directional derivatives.</strong> Forward-mode automatic differentiation computes exact directional derivatives without a reverse-mode pass. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-baydin2018ad" id="citation-baydin2018ad-1">Baydin et al., 2018</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-baydin2022forward" id="citation-baydin2022forward-1">Baydin et al., 2022</a></span> Subsequent work reduces variance by changing direction choice, localizing objectives, or perturbing activations rather than full parameter vectors. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-fournier2023forward" id="citation-fournier2023forward-1">Fournier et al., 2023</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-ren2023forwardlocal" id="citation-ren2023forwardlocal-1">Ren et al., 2023</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-bacho2024forward" id="citation-bacho2024forward-1">Bacho &amp; Chu, 2024</a></span> This literature directly motivates the article's distinction between directional <strong>measurement exactness</strong> and <strong>coverage</strong> of the hidden-credit space.</p><p><strong>Perturbation-based local credit.</strong> Weight perturbation and node perturbation estimate credit from changes in global outcome produced by local interventions. Analyses show that the intervention space affects learning efficiency and variance. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-werfel2005curves" id="citation-werfel2005curves-1">Werfel et al., 2005</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-dalm2026node" id="citation-dalm2026node-1">Dalm et al., 2023</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-zuge2023perturbation" id="citation-zuge2023perturbation-1">Züge et al., 2023</a></span> Activation-Space ALOPEX belongs to this broad class because it moves the intervention from parameters into hidden activations while preserving a downstream consequence measurement.</p><p><strong>Fixed and learned feedback pathways.</strong> Feedback alignment shows that useful error signals can be transmitted through feedback weights that are not equal to the forward weights; direct feedback alignment routes output error directly to hidden layers through fixed random projections. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-lillicrap2016feedback" id="citation-lillicrap2016feedback-1">Lillicrap et al., 2014</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-nokland2016direct" id="citation-nokland2016direct-1">Nøkland, 2016</a></span> Synthetic gradients instead train a predictor of downstream credit so modules can update without waiting for the complete downstream computation. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-jaderberg2017synthetic" id="citation-jaderberg2017synthetic-1">Jaderberg et al., 2017</a></span> Learned Credit-Map Hybrid is closest to this learned-credit family: it uses sparse hidden JVP observations to update a reusable output-to-hidden operator, then applies AdamW to the reconstructed parameter evidence.</p><p><strong>Energy-based and predictive-coding methods.</strong> Equilibrium propagation computes objective gradients in an energy-based network through free and weakly clamped relaxation phases rather than a conventional reverse-mode backward pass. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-scellier2017equilibrium" id="citation-scellier2017equilibrium-1">Scellier &amp; Bengio, 2017</a></span> Predictive-coding work subsequently showed how local inference dynamics can approximate backpropagated gradients on differentiable computation graphs. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-millidge2020predictive" id="citation-millidge2020predictive-1">Millidge et al., 2020</a></span> PC-ALM extends this line with layer-local primal-dual dynamics and explicit dual variables that accumulate constraint errors. In linear networks, Seely and Gould show that the equilibrium multipliers recover exact backpropagation credit; their nonlinear experiments cover residual MLPs through depth 128. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-seely2026pcalm" id="citation-seely2026pcalm-1">Seely &amp; Gould, 2026</a></span></p><p><strong>Local objectives that change the training problem.</strong> Forward-Forward removes the conventional backward pass by training each layer with positive and negative data under a local goodness objective. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-hinton2022forwardforward" id="citation-hinton2022forwardforward-1">Hinton, 2022</a></span> This is a different methodological choice from estimating the gradient of the original supervised objective. It is included because it demonstrates that some backprop-free methods change the objective rather than only changing how credit for a fixed objective is transported.</p><p>The literature therefore separates at least three questions that are sometimes conflated: whether derivatives are available, whether the global supervised objective is retained, and whether credit is transported globally or generated from local dynamics. The ALOPEX branches in this article occupy different points in that space: Two-Timescale Activation-Space ALOPEX is derivative-free, Matched-JVP Control and Learned Credit-Map Hybrid use directional derivatives, and Learned Credit-Map Hybrid additionally learns a credit map and uses AdamW actuation.</p><h3>Terminology used throughout the article</h3><div class="interactive-data compact" <strong>Activation-JVP ALOPEX</strong> is the hidden-activation branch; <strong>Matched-JVP Control</strong> is the controlled sensor substitution for Two-Timescale Activation-Space ALOPEX; and <strong>Learned Credit-Map Hybrid</strong> is the learned-credit method with AdamW actuation. Legacy version IDs are retained only for provenance.</p></section>

:::interactive alopex-reader.js Evidence and provenance
:::

###### Thread I — A correlative learner meets credit assignment

<section id="thread-one" class="paper-section" data-section="02" aria-label="Thread I — A correlative learner meets credit assignment"><aside class="in-brief" aria-label="Section summary"><span>In brief</span><div><p>The thesis-era starting point exposes a structural limitation. A global change in objective can identify whether a joint intervention changed the loss without identifying which coordinates deserve credit. The recovered Mini-Batch Correlative ALOPEX translation (legacy v8) also narrows the implementation claim: it uses gradient-seeded directions through epoch zero by default, and its default model is logistic regression rather than a hidden network. Those are code observations from the recovered translation, not proof that the exact submitted TensorFlow notebook behaved identically. The written thesis is now available as a separate primary source. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-roy2023thesis" id="citation-roy2023thesis-2">Roy, 2023</a></span></p></div></aside><p>A small perturbation of parameters <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>θ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta\theta</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span differentiable loss approximately by</p><span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" display="block"><semantics><mrow><mi class="mord">.</span></span></span></span></span><p>Even if the scalar <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta L</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em"></span><span class="mord">Δ</span><span class="mord mathnormal">L</span></span></span></span> is measured perfectly, it is one inner product. Many possible gradients are consistent with that one number. This is an identifiability problem before it is a noise problem.</p><p>SPSA made an important version of this trade explicit: two function evaluations can estimate a stochastic gradient without a query count that grows directly with parameter dimension. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-spall1992spsa" id="citation-spall1992spsa-2">Spall, 1992</a></span> That sits inside a much broader stochastic-approximation and gradient-free lineage, from Robbins–Monro and Kiefer–Wolfowitz to modern random-direction methods. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-robbins1951stochastic" id="citation-robbins1951stochastic-1">Robbins &amp; Monro, 1951</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-kiefer1952stochastic" id="citation-kiefer1952stochastic-1">Kiefer &amp; Wolfowitz, 1952</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-nesterov2017random" id="citation-nesterov2017random-2">Nesterov &amp; Spokoiny, 2017</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-liu2020primer" id="citation-liu2020primer-2">Liu et al., 2020</a></span> But constant query count is not constant information. As dimensionality rises, the variance and geometry of the estimator become decisive.</p><p>The recovered Mini-Batch ALOPEX-BIA translation in this repository is treated as a starting instrument with a limited view of causality. It keeps coordinate-specific direction state, but a scalar consequence controls stochastic decisions across a large parameter set. It is not correct to say that every coordinate moves identically. It is correct to say that the observed consequence is globally compressed.</p><aside id="claim-v8-boundary" class="claim claim-limitation" data-claim-kind="limitation"><header><span>Limitation / evidence boundary</span><code>claim-v8-boundary</code></header><div class="claim-body"><p>The MSc thesis is available as a primary source, but the exact submitted TensorFlow implementation remains unresolved. The recovered Mini-Batch Correlative ALOPEX PyTorch translation can support code-only observations about its information access, ordering, objective, architecture, and default state.</p></div></aside><section class="science-panel v8-audit" aria-labelledby="v8-audit-title"><header><span>Recovered-code audit · not thesis attribution</span><h3 id="v8-audit-title">Eight source observations define what the historical starting point actually supports.</h3></header><div class="science-table-wrap"><table><thead><tr><th>ID</th><th>Observation</th><th>Recovered-source finding</th><th>Interpretation boundary</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><th scope="row">RC-01</th><td>Gradient access</td><td>Epoch 0 seeds directions with loss.backward() on every minibatch by default.</td><td>Recovered translation is not strictly gradient-free by default.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">RC-02</th><td>Spatial credit</td><td>One global probability controls coordinate-specific stored directions and random keep/flip draws.</td><td>Global probability is not dense per-parameter loss credit.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">RC-03</th><td>Loss pairing</td><td>Pre/post losses are evaluated on the same minibatch.</td><td>The suspected cross-batch subtraction defect is not supported by this translation.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">RC-04</th><td>Feedback timing</td><td>Previous-step loss difference controls the next movement.</td><td>Delay is verified source ordering; whether it is harmful is a separate empirical question.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">RC-05</th><td>Initialization</td><td>Loss difference, probability, denominator and random directions are explicitly initialized.</td><td>Undefined initialization is not supported for this translation.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">RC-06</th><td>Objective</td><td>Manual softmax + 1e-5 before log differs from stable standard cross-entropy.</td><td>Objective identity must be held fixed in comparisons.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">RC-07</th><td>Architecture</td><td>Default recovered model is 784→10 logistic regression, 7,850 parameters.</td><td>It cannot establish hidden-layer credit assignment.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">RC-08</th><td>Protocol</td><td>Defaults specify seeds/epochs/test evaluation but are configuration, not executed-run evidence.</td><td>Equal epochs do not imply equal compute or repeated-run uncertainty.</td></tr></tbody></table></div></section><p>The recovered translation also corrected one suspected defect in the historical story. Its pre/post losses are evaluated on the <strong>same mini-batch</strong>. The older hypothesis that it simply subtracts losses from unrelated consecutive batches is not supported by this source. The remaining timing issue is different: the measured consequence influences later stochastic motion, so the feedback can become stale as parameters and batches change.</p><p>That distinction becomes important later. One can improve statistical pairing without solving credit assignment. One can eliminate a timing confound without increasing spatial resolution. One can make a signal cleaner and still ask it to answer an underdetermined question.</p><aside class="paper-margin-note"><span>Evidence discipline</span><p>Statements about Mini-Batch Correlative ALOPEX are restricted to observations supported by the recovered code. The written thesis supports thesis-level claims; implementation-specific claims require the submitted notebook. Retrospective explanations of why later branches are relevant are labeled as retrospective.</p></aside><p>Subsequent branches are compared by their explicit information contracts: <strong>what is measured, where the intervention is applied, what state persists, and how that state produces an update</strong>.</p><aside class="paper-margin-note"><span>Lineage gap</span><p>The reconstructed article is not a contiguous version log. The verified narrative moves from Block-Replay ALOPEX (legacy v13) to Activation-Space ALOPEX (legacy v43) because the archive does not currently support a complete, source-audited account of every intervening version number. Versions 14–42 are therefore <strong>unresolved lineage here</strong>, not silently reclassified as failures. Recovering a branch later can refine the map without changing the evidence already frozen for Block-Replay ALOPEX (legacy v13) or Activation-Space ALOPEX (legacy v43).</p></aside></section>

###### Gradient-Seeded → Block-Replay — measurement repairs reveal a credit problem

<section id="repair-chain" class="paper-section" data-section="03" aria-label="Gradient-Seeded → Block-Replay — measurement repairs reveal a credit problem"><aside class="in-brief" aria-label="Section summary"><span>In brief</span><div><p>Four recovered notebooks form the bridge between Mini-Batch Correlative ALOPEX and Activation-Space ALOPEX. Gradient-Seeded ALOPEX makes the warmup access contract explicit. Historical-Credit ALOPEX replaces explicit probes with lagged loss-change credit. Same-Batch Replay ALOPEX repairs the pairing by replaying the previous batch. Block-Replay ALOPEX localizes the intervention to one layer or block. The sequence repeatedly makes consequence easier to interpret and repeatedly discovers that interpretation is still too coarse.</p></div></aside><h3>Gradient-Seeded ALOPEX: make information access explicit</h3><p>The recovered Gradient-Seeded ALOPEX notebook (legacy Gradient-Seeded ALOPEX) begins from a simple correction. If gradient information is used throughout the warmup epoch, implement and describe that honestly. Its warmup direction is</p><span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" mechanism than the methodological lesson. “Gradient-free” and “backprop-free” are not decorative labels. They are access contracts. A method that consults a gradient, even only during initialization or warmup, occupies a different problem class from a method that can operate through score-only evaluation.</p><h3>Historical-Credit ALOPEX: make the measurement cheaper</h3><p>Antithetic measurement can be expensive because it asks the objective to evaluate positive and negative interventions. Historical-Credit ALOPEX asked whether temporal credit could replace those extra measurements. Instead of explicit plus/minus consequence, it used historical loss change to credit the previous update:</p><span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" display="block"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>Z</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mo>−</mo><mi resulting loss difference is not attributable only to that update. The model changed, the sampled examples can change, and a global scalar still has to assign credit across many degrees of freedom.</p><aside id="claim-v11-negative" class="claim claim-derived" data-claim-kind="derived"><header><span>Derived / analytical conclusion</span><code>claim-v11-negative</code></header><div class="claim-body"><p>Historical-Credit ALOPEX tested whether <strong>temporal correlation could substitute for explicit intervention measurement</strong>. Under the recorded protocol, the resulting historical loss signal remained too indirect to provide reliable spatial credit.</p></div></aside><h3>Same-Batch Replay ALOPEX: replay the same evidence</h3><p>Same-Batch Replay ALOPEX repairs the temporal measurement by storing the previous batch's loss before an update, then replaying that same batch after the update:</p><span can still fail to identify which layer's movement helped. Same-Batch Replay ALOPEX makes the consequence more interpretable while leaving spatial attribution compressed.</p><h3>Block-Replay ALOPEX: restrict what is allowed to move</h3><p>Block-Replay ALOPEX addresses that ambiguity by updating only one layer or block per mini-batch. Now the replay reward can be associated with the component that changed:</p><span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" style="height:0.15em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord">Δ</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.13889em">W</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">ℓ</span><span class="mpunct mtight">,</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span><span class="mbin mtight">−</span><span class="mord mtight">1</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord">.</span></span></span></span></span><p>This changes the intervention design by allowing only one layer or block to move, reducing the number of simultaneous causes associated with the replayed scalar.</p><p>That is the bridge to the later work. The use of persistent state and separated update rates also sits near the historical two-timescale ALOPEX and stochastic-approximation literature, though the recovered Historical-Credit through Block-Replay rules are not identical to those convergence settings. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-sastry2002timescale" id="citation-sastry2002timescale-1">Sastry et al., 2002</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-borkar1997timescales" id="citation-borkar1997timescales-1">Borkar, 1997</a></span></p><aside id="claim-repair-chain" class="claim claim-derived" data-claim-kind="derived"><header><span>Derived / analytical conclusion</span><code>claim-repair-chain</code></header><div class="claim-body"><p>Across Historical-Credit, Same-Batch Replay, and Block-Replay ALOPEX, each repair improves <strong>causal interpretability of the consequence</strong>: historical global signal → same-batch replay → one-block intervention. The remaining pressure is therefore naturally expressed as a question of credit space.</p></div></aside><p>This pattern is broader than ALOPEX. Derivative-free optimization becomes more tractable when structure limits the effective search space. General DFO surveys and random-direction analyses make clear that oracle structure, dimension, and direction design are part of the method contract. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-larson2019dfo" id="citation-larson2019dfo-2">Larson et al., 2019</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-kozak2021orthogonal" id="citation-kozak2021orthogonal-1">Kozak et al., 2021</a></span> Random subspace results distinguish raw parameter count from useful optimization dimension. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-li2018intrinsic" id="citation-li2018intrinsic-1">Li et al., 2018</a></span> DeepZero later makes a similar systems point for large models: scalable zeroth-order training requires coordinate, sparsity, and feature structure rather than treating the network as an undifferentiated flat vector. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-chen2024deepzero" id="citation-chen2024deepzero-2">Chen et al., 2024</a></span></p><p>The next recovered branch changed the intervention space from parameters to hidden representations.</p></section>

###### Thread II — move the intervention into function space

<section id="thread-two" class="paper-section" data-section="04" aria-label="Thread II — move the intervention into function space"><aside class="in-brief" aria-label="Section summary"><span>In brief</span><div><p>Activation-Space ALOPEX (legacy v43) changes the geometry of the problem. Rather than perturbing every weight as one giant causal field, it perturbs a hidden activation or preactivation site, observes downstream consequence, and reconstructs local parameter evidence from cached activity. Activation-JVP ALOPEX then tests the same interface after replacing finite consequences with exact directional derivatives.</p></div></aside><p>Automatic differentiation separates two operations that ordinary backprop often fuses in practice: computing a derivative and choosing how that derivative travels through a network. Forward mode propagates tangents from inputs toward outputs; reverse mode propagates cotangents from outputs toward inputs. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-baydin2018ad" id="citation-baydin2018ad-2">Baydin et al., 2018</a></span></p><p>For a scalar objective <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">L</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">L</span></span></span></span> and direction <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">u</span></span></span></span>, a JVP can provide the exact directional derivative</p><span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" display="block"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>J</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>θ</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mi>u</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">∇</mi><mi>θ</mi></msub><msup><mi>L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">⊤</mi></msup><mi>u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">.</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">J_L(\theta)u = \nabla_\theta L^\top u.</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.09618em">J</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0962em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0491em;vertical-align:-0.15em"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord">∇</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" mathnormal">L</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8991em"><span style="top:-3.113em;margin-right:0.05em"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">⊤</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord mathnormal">u</span><span class="mord">.</span></span></span></span></span><p>That coefficient is exact for the chosen direction. It is not the full gradient unless enough independent directions are measured or some additional structural assumption reconstructs the missing components.</p><p>Forward-gradient work makes this distinction explicit. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-baydin2022forward" id="citation-baydin2022forward-2">Baydin et al., 2022</a></span> Subsequent forward-gradient studies focus directly on the variance and alignment gap that appears when directional measurements replace reverse-mode credit. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-fournier2023forward" id="citation-fournier2023forward-2">Fournier et al., 2023</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-bacho2024forward" id="citation-bacho2024forward-2">Bacho &amp; Chu, 2024</a></span> Related local-learning work shows that moving perturbations into activation space can reduce variance and shorten the credit path. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-ren2023forwardlocal" id="citation-ren2023forwardlocal-2">Ren et al., 2023</a></span></p><p>Activation-Space ALOPEX belongs in this second family of ideas, while retaining ALOPEX-style persistence. Perturbation learning has a longer neural lineage of its own—weight perturbation, node perturbation, and dynamic perturbation all ask how much useful credit can be recovered from consequences without ordinary reverse-mode transport. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-werfel2005curves" id="citation-werfel2005curves-2">Werfel et al., 2005</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-dalm2026node" id="citation-dalm2026node-2">Dalm et al., 2023</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-zuge2023perturbation" id="citation-zuge2023perturbation-2">Züge et al., 2023</a></span></p><p>Its conceptual move is:</p><ol>
<li>intervene at a hidden site;</li>
<li>measure the downstream loss consequence of that intervention;</li>
<li>preserve per-example consequence rather than collapsing the batch too early;</li>
<li>use cached local activity to reconstruct parameter evidence;</li>
<li>accumulate persistent correlation over repeated measurements.</li>
</ol><p>The frozen Activation-Space ALOPEX mathematical contract makes that sequence explicit: hidden interventions preserve per-example consequence, cached local inputs realize hidden credit into parameter evidence, and the probe radius follows activation scale.</p><section class="scientific-derivation" aria-label="Intervene in hidden space, then reconstruct local parameter evidence."><header><span>Activation-Space ALOPEX · frozen mathematical contract</span><h4>Intervene in hidden space, then reconstruct local parameter evidence.</h4></header><div transport is heuristic rather than covariance-exact.</p></div></div></section><p>The persistent C/S state can denoise repeated evidence, but after a change in the local objective, the same memory can continue to justify updates using stale credit. That tradeoff follows from the documented recurrence; it does not require a toy simulation to establish.</p><p>The four-axis decomposition is the comparison framework for the rest of the article. A method may change one axis without changing the others. A complete-method result that changes all four axes cannot attribute the improvement to one mechanism.</p><p>The later matched sensor control isolates the measurement channel while retaining the repaired C/S comparison target.</p></section>

###### Exactness is not coverage

<section id="exactness" class="paper-section" data-section="05" aria-label="Exactness is not coverage"><aside class="in-brief" aria-label="Section summary"><span>In brief</span><div><p>The central conceptual result is negative and simple: replacing a finite directional measurement with an exact JVP did not consistently improve the repaired C/S learner in the short matched pilot. The result does not say derivatives are useless. It says that cleaner coefficients do not create missing directions.</p></div></aside><p>Suppose the useful hidden credit is <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">g</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.03588em">g</span></span></span></span>, but the learner observes only directions in the columns of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">U</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.10903em">U</span></span></span></span>. Even if every directional coefficient is exact, the learner can reconstruct at most the component of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">g</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" class="mord">.</span></span></span></span></span><p>Exact measurement can improve the first term. It does not reveal the second.</p><p>The distinction is algebraic: exactness describes the directional coefficient, whereas coverage describes the span of measured directions. Exactness is a property of the <strong>coefficient</strong>. Coverage is a property of the <strong>measurement basis</strong>.</p><p>A finite central difference and an exact JVP differ in coefficient error, but both still observe only the chosen directions. The parallel parameter-space Parameter-JVP branch makes the distinction explicit through a finite-rank coverage operator.</p><section class="scientific-derivation" aria-label="Exact directional class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.07847em;">I</span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mord mathnormal">d</span></span></span></span></span></div><p>The frozen globally normalized Rademacher construction has a constant expectation scale.</p></div></div></section><p><strong>Measurement exactness and reconstruction coverage are separate mathematical objects.</strong></p><aside id="claim-exactness-coverage" class="claim claim-frozen" data-claim-kind="frozen"><header><span>Frozen empirical evidence</span><code>claim-exactness-coverage</code></header><div class="claim-body"><p>In the recorded short matched mechanism pilot, Matched-JVP Control replaced the repaired Two-Timescale Activation-Space ALOPEX finite sensor with matched exact directional JVP measurements while retaining the repaired C/S memory and custom actuator as the comparison target. The exact sensor did <strong>not</strong> consistently improve C/S under that protocol.</p></div></aside><p>This result is restricted to the recorded short matched pilot and does not establish a general claim about JVPs in ALOPEX variants. The pilot is short, uses a small number of seeds, and tests one design. Persistent filtering, normalization, clipping, direction selection, and learning horizon can all mediate whether cleaner measurements alter final behavior.</p><p>Under the stated mechanism hypothesis, this result reduced support for sensor noise as the primary bottleneck.</p><p>Under the tested sensor-noise hypothesis, a matched exact directional measurement was expected to improve the repaired C/S comparison. No consistent improvement was observed across the two recorded calibration seeds. The subsequent branch therefore changed the persistent representation by learning reusable credit structure.</p><p>There is another subtlety. A normalized directional estimator can be unbiased in expectation over a random direction distribution while any one realized low-rank span remains incomplete. An expectation statement does not make one finite set of measured directions informationally complete. Online subspace-estimation work such as PETRELS is relevant precisely because a useful low-rank basis must itself be estimated and tracked when the underlying geometry changes. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-chi2013petrels" id="citation-chi2013petrels-1">Chi et al., 2012</a></span></p><aside id="claim-coverage-priority" class="claim claim-derived" data-claim-kind="derived"><header><span>Derived / analytical conclusion</span><code>claim-coverage-priority</code></header><div class="claim-body"><p>After the matched sensor substitution, the most informative next question is no longer “can the coefficient become more exact?” It is “can the learner infer useful unmeasured credit from reusable structure?”</p></div></aside><p>This result motivated the subsequent learned-credit branch, which changed the persistent representation rather than only the sensor.</p></section>

###### Thread III — learn the credit map

<section id="thread-three" class="paper-section" data-section="06" aria-label="Thread III — learn the credit map"><aside class="in-brief" aria-label="Section summary"><span>In brief</span><div><p>Learned Credit-Map Hybrid changes the persistent object. Instead of only smoothing current evidence, it learns an output-to-hidden credit operator (A). The old (A) predicts current hidden credit; sparse exact measurements then update (A) for future steps. Local parameter evidence is actuated with AdamW. Learned Credit-Map Hybrid produced the highest recorded feed-forward neural-training quality among the branches in this program, while also changing the persistent representation and actuator relative to earlier ALOPEX variants.</p></div></aside><p>For example <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math It trains future credit.</p><p>This makes <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">A</span></span></span></span> qualitatively different from C/S. C/S integrates evidence along repeated directions. <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">A</span></span></span></span> is a learned map from output condition to hidden credit, conceptually closer to learned/synthetic credit mechanisms than to a pure stochastic correlation trace. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-jaderberg2017synthetic" id="citation-jaderberg2017synthetic-2">Jaderberg et al., 2017</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-maheswaranathan2019guided" id="citation-maheswaranathan2019guided-1">Maheswaranathan et al., 2018</a></span></p><p>Predicted hidden credit is realized locally into parameter evidence, after which Learned Credit-Map Hybrid switches actuator families entirely: reconstructed evidence drives Adam first/second moments, bias correction, and decoupled weight decay.</p><section c-1 5-5 9-11 9h-2L532 67 19 159h-2c-5 0-9-4-11-9l-5-22c-1-6 2-12 8-13z"></path></svg></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2806em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.93em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose nulldelimiter"></span></span></span></span></span></span></div><p>Decoupled weight decay and adaptive descent complete the actuator.</p></div></div></section><p>This is why Learned Credit-Map Hybrid cannot be described as “the same ALOPEX state with a better sensor.” It changed the persistent representation and the actuator. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-kingma2015adam" id="citation-kingma2015adam-1">Kingma &amp; Ba, 2015</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-loshchilov2019adamw" id="citation-loshchilov2019adamw-1">Loshchilov &amp; Hutter, 2019</a></span></p><aside id="claim-hfdjp-identity" class="claim claim-derived" data-claim-kind="derived"><header><span>Derived / analytical conclusion</span><code>claim-hfdjp-identity</code></header><div class="claim-body"><p>Learned Credit-Map Hybrid is classified here as an <strong>ALOPEX-inspired learned-credit hybrid</strong>: sparse forward directional measurements supervise a persistent credit model, while AdamW actuates the resulting parameter evidence. It is not modern 2T-ALOPEX and Adam's second moment is not ALOPEX (S).</p></div></aside><p>The recorded distinction remains explicit: the old learned operator predicts credit for the current step; new directional observations supervise the operator used on subsequent steps. No browser micro-run is presented as canonical training evidence.</p><p>This identity distinction is required for causal attribution. Relative to repaired C/S, Learned Credit-Map Hybrid changes the form of persistent memory, the supervision of that memory, the current-credit predictor, and the actuator. The complete-method result is evidence about Learned Credit-Map Hybrid as a combined method; it is not a causal ablation isolating the Jacobian prior.</p><p>The canonical CIFAR result remains bounded. In the canonical shallow residual-MLP comparison, Learned Credit-Map Hybrid stayed within the project's predeclared five-percentage-point practical gap criterion relative to AdamW. It did not close the gap, establish equivalence, or win on accuracy.</p><p>The canonical comparison contains only three paired training seeds. The recorded observations and their arithmetic summaries must therefore be interpreted as small-sample evidence, not as an equivalence claim.</p><aside id="claim-hfdjp-bounded" class="claim claim-frozen" data-claim-kind="frozen"><header><span>Frozen empirical evidence</span><code>claim-hfdjp-bounded</code></header><div class="claim-body"><p>The recorded Learned Credit-Map Hybrid experiments demonstrate forward-only hidden-credit learning in the tested residual-MLP family. They do <strong>not</strong> establish AdamW parity, Transformer scaling, universal memory savings, positive CNN transfer, or a general replacement for reverse-mode training.</p></div></aside><p>A learned credit map also creates new failure modes. It can be stale. It can average away example-conditioned structure. It can be biased by its own prediction family. A shared output-to-hidden operator can fail when two examples share predictor inputs but require different hidden credit.</p><p>Learned Credit-Map Hybrid combines a learned credit model with sparse directional supervision and AdamW actuation. This places it in a broader class of methods that alter the credit pathway, including feedback alignment, direct feedback alignment, and synthetic-gradient methods. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-lillicrap2016feedback" id="citation-lillicrap2016feedback-2">Lillicrap et al., 2014</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-nokland2016direct" id="citation-nokland2016direct-2">Nøkland, 2016</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-jaderberg2017synthetic" id="citation-jaderberg2017synthetic-3">Jaderberg et al., 2017</a></span> Relative to the historical ALOPEX formulations, the mechanism now contains additional learned state and a different actuator.</p></section>

###### Scaling and systems — where the remaining gap becomes structural

<section id="scaling" class="paper-section" data-section="07" aria-label="Scaling and systems — where the remaining gap becomes structural"><aside class="in-brief" aria-label="Section summary"><span>In brief</span><div><p>Width and depth did not behave symmetrically. Wider tested residual MLPs could remain inside the project's five-point engineering criterion, while the depth-32 cases crossed it. The corrected resident-step benchmark also remained materially slower than optimized AdamW. These are not side notes: they define the boundary of the neural-training claim.</p></div></aside><p>A fixed directional rank sounds attractive because it avoids storing or measuring a full dense Jacobian. But fixed rank does not imply fixed difficulty as depth grows.</p><p>A direction injected into an early hidden site must influence later transformations before the output can measure its consequence. Repeating that process across multiple sites creates suffix work. A persistent learned prior must also remain useful as the intervening representation changes.</p><p>The recorded scaling family shows an asymmetry: the tested width increases retained smaller Learned Credit-Map Hybrid/AdamW gaps than the tested depth increases.</p><h3>Methodology: what counts as one experiment</h3><p>The empirical programme contains several different statistical objects. The <strong>training seed</strong> is the independent unit for the paired Learned Credit-Map Hybrid/AdamW quality summaries. Per-epoch evaluations from one trajectory are repeated measurements, not extra samples. Checkpoint-identical backend reruns repeat an outcome rather than creating a new one. Timing windows measure systems variance and are not pooled with training seeds. The historical CNN record contains replicate aggregates rather than recoverable paired seed identities.</p><p>For the canonical paired analysis, the training-seed difference and its exploratory Student-t interval are defined explicitly rather than inferred from unpaired method spreads.</p><section class="scientific-derivation" aria-label="The statistical unit is the training seed, not the epoch."><header><span>Methodology · paired quality analysis</span><h4>The statistical unit is the training seed, not the epoch.</h4></header><div class="scientific-derivation-body"><div conditional on the recorded paired design.</p></div></div></section><p>For the canonical cohort, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n=3</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">n</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em"></span><span class="mord">3</span></span></span></span>. This is a training-seed interval conditional on one shared test set. It is not an equivalence test, a population confidence interval over new datasets, or justification for treating the scaling ladder as one larger sample.</p><p>Matching is equally important. Canonical pairs share initialization, first augmented batch, architecture, split, horizon, and step count. The narrative pilot and latest GPU pairs additionally record the full training-batch sequence hash. Equal steps still do not imply equal objective queries, JVP work, wall time, or memory.</p><section class="science-panel protocol-panel" aria-labelledby="protocol-title"><header><span>Methodology · experimental units</span><h3 id="protocol-title">Do not pool experiments that answer different questions.</h3></header><div class="science-table-wrap"><table><thead><tr><th>Cohort</th><th>Architecture</th><th>Seeds / unit</th><th>Horizon</th><th>Evaluation</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><th scope="row">canonical-shallow</th><td>residual MLP; 19,500 steps</td><td>official test</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">scaling-depth16</th><td>residual MLP; W512, 16 hidden stages</td><td>3 paired seeds</td><td>50 epochs; 19,500 steps</td><td>official test</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">scaling-depth32</th><td>residual MLP; Epochs inside one trajectory are repeated measurements, checkpoint-identical backend reruns are not new samples, and timing windows are not training seeds.</p></section><h3>Canonical result: keep the seeds visible</h3><section class="science-panel canonical-seeds" aria-labelledby="canonical-seeds-title"><header><span>Frozen empirical result · three paired seeds</span><h3 id="canonical-seeds-title">The canonical shallow result is a small-n paired experiment.</h3></header><div class="seed-summary"><div><span>Learned Credit-Map Hybrid</span><strong>57.79%</strong><small>SD 0.50 pp</small></div><div><span>AdamW</span><strong>60.93%</strong><small>SD 0.34 pp</small></div><div><span>Paired gap</span><strong>3.13 pp</strong><small>95% exploratory interval 2.711–3.556 pp</small></div></div><div class="seed-pairs" role="table" aria-label="Canonical paired seed outcomes"><div class="seed-row head" role="row"><span role="columnheader">Seed</span><span role="columnheader">Learned Credit-Map Hybrid</span><span role="columnheader">AdamW</span><span role="columnheader">Gap</span><span role="columnheader">5 pp gate</span></div><div class="seed-row" role="row"><strong role="rowheader">1523</strong><span role="cell">57.25%</span><span role="cell">60.55%</span><span role="cell">3.30 pp</span><b role="cell">met</b></div><div class="seed-row" role="row"><strong role="rowheader">1524</strong><span role="cell">58.24%</span><span role="cell">61.20%</span><span role="cell">2.96 pp</span><b role="cell">met</b></div><div class="seed-row" role="row"><strong role="rowheader">1525</strong><span role="cell">57.89%</span><span role="cell">61.03%</span><span role="cell">3.14 pp</span><b role="cell">met</b></div></div><div class="gate-strip"><span>strong criterion ≤2 pp</span><i><b style="width:52.2222222222222%"></b></i><span>practical criterion ≤5 pp</span></div><p class="science-note">Passing the 5 pp engineering gate does not establish parity or equivalence. pp.</p></div></section><p>The observed widening quality gap from depth 7 to 16 to 32 is frozen evidence. A proposed layerwise credit-attenuation explanation remains a hypothesis: this archive does not contain recovered per-layer Learned Credit-Map Hybrid credit telemetry that would establish that mechanism.</p><section class="scale-trajectory" aria-labelledby="scale-trajectory-title"><header class="scale-trajectory-head"><div><span>Experiment scale trajectory · parameter count</span><h3 id="scale-trajectory-title">33 parameters → 113.3 million</h3><p>The feed-forward programme expanded by <strong>3.43 million×</strong>, covering <strong>6.54 orders of magnitude</strong>. The final point is SLM-scale by parameter count only—not by architecture, task difficulty, or demonstrated capability.</p></div><div class="scale-trajectory-stat"><span>largest executed XOR</b><span>Prove the correlative update can solve a nonlinear toy network at all.</span><em>33 params</em></li><li><b>2 · MNIST</b><span>Move credit into hidden activation space and train a ~million-parameter MLP.</span><em>814K</em></li><li><b>3 · CIFAR W512</b><span>First canonical real-image residual-MLP Learned Credit-Map Hybrid comparison with matched AdamW.</span><em>3.15M</em></li><li><b>4 · Width scaling</b><span>Hold seven hidden stages and expand W512 → W1024 → W2048.</span><em>3.15M → 31.49M</em></li><li><b>5 · Depth scaling</b><span>Hold W512 and expand d7 → d16 → d32; quality gap worsens with depth.</span><em>3.15M → 9.72M</em></li><li><b>6 · 100M+ mechanism scale</b><span>Test whether low-rank persistent function-space credit still executes when the MLP enters a small-language-model parameter regime.</span><em>113.3M</em></li></ol></div><details class="depth-branch"><summary>Depth branch: parameter count was not the whole scaling problem</summary><div><article><span>W512 · d16 · 5.52M</span><strong>3072 → 512 → 15×512 → 10</strong><p>Learned Credit-Map Hybrid 58.33% vs AdamW 62.70%; gap 4.37 pp.</p><small>frozen 3-seed scaling result</small></article><article><span>W512 · d32 · 9.72M</span><strong>3072 → 512 → 31×512 → 10</strong><p>Completed later depth-32 cohorts crossed the 5 pp practical gap boundary.</p><small>separate completed deep snapshot</small></article></div></details><p class="scale-caveat">Parameter count measures scale, not equivalence of evidence. The Learned Credit-Map Hybrid CIFAR points are full quality experiments; the 113.3M point demonstrates executable mechanism/system scale and must not be read as a 113M convergence or AdamW-competitive result.</p></section><p>The scale chart makes another distinction visible. The programme did reach a <strong>113.3M-parameter residual MLP</strong>, roughly the parameter regime of a small language model, but that endpoint was a mechanism/scaling execution rather than a full convergence benchmark. The frozen CIFAR Learned Credit-Map Hybrid family contains the recorded quality comparisons; the 113.3M-parameter endpoint is classified separately as mechanism-scale execution. Parameter count therefore records how far the mechanism was pushed; it does not by itself upgrade the evidentiary strength of the result.</p><h3>Scaling is not competitiveness</h3><p>The 113.3M result answers an engineering question: <strong>can the intervention, persistent state, and local update machinery still be expressed and executed when the MLP becomes very large?</strong> In that bounded sense, yes. The low-rank function-space state avoided a parameter-sized persistent C/S tensor, the probe count remained fixed, and the implementation could operate in a parameter regime associated with small language models.</p><p>But competitive neural training asks a harder question: <strong>does the credit signal remain accurate enough to train deep networks near a strong first-order reference?</strong> The internal evidence is much weaker there. In the archived Learned Credit-Map Hybrid family, the canonical shallow case was roughly three percentage points behind matched AdamW, depth 16 widened to roughly four and a half points, and the completed depth-32 cohorts crossed the declared five-point practical gap boundary. The 113.3M mechanism-scale run does not reverse that trend because it was not a 113M convergence benchmark.</p><p>The external PC-ALM comparison is evaluated in Thread IV because it addresses the same local-credit problem under a different method, task protocol, and compute contract. It is not part of the frozen ALOPEX evidence.</p><section class="mechanism-lab evidence-replay" aria-labelledby="evidence-replay-title"><header class="mechanism-lab-head"><div><span>Canonical replay · verified evidence master</span><h3 id="evidence-replay-title">Compare the evidence without hiding neighboring results.</h3><p>Tables are primary.
