data-claim-kind="frozen"><header><span>Frozen empirical evidence</span><code>claim-systems-boundary</code></header><div class="claim-body"><p>The archived systems evidence shows that removing reverse-mode hidden-credit traversal did not reduce resident-step latency below AdamW in the measured configuration: Learned Credit-Map Hybrid was approximately 2.47× slower.</p></div></aside><p>Removing a conventional backward pass therefore did not establish a lower complete-step cost for Learned Credit-Map Hybrid.</p><p>Forward-only methods can trade reverse-mode memory for additional forward evaluations, tangent propagation, probe calls, state, offloading, or communication. Classical checkpointing already illustrates that memory and recomputation form a systems trade rather than a single scalar notion of efficiency. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-chen2016checkpoint" id="citation-chen2016checkpoint-1">Chen et al., 2016</a></span> MeZO reports an in-place zeroth-order implementation for large pretrained models with inference-like memory use and support for nondifferentiable task objectives. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-malladi2023mezo" id="citation-malladi2023mezo-2">Malladi et al., 2023</a></span> But that systems result does not transfer automatically to Learned Credit-Map Hybrid, which retains a different set of states and derivative requirements.</p><p>A compute comparison therefore requires more than equal optimizer steps. Depending on the deployment question, the relevant budget may be objective queries, forward-equivalent work, JVPs, wall time, accelerator memory, host memory, or communication.</p><p>The same care applies to numerical validation. Some historical deep BF16 audits failed their relative-error gate; the isolated FP32 deep experiment passed its own numerical check. A numerical pass permits interpretation of that experiment. It does not convert a quality miss into a performance success.</p><p>Finally, the historical CNN extension recorded a large negative gap under a different branch/protocol. It is retained as historical negative evidence and is not classified as Learned Credit-Map Hybrid CNN evidence.</p><section class="science-panel rejected-ledger" aria-labelledby="rejected-title"><header><span>Negative results · retained in main text</span><h3 id="rejected-title">Rejected hypotheses are part of the result.</h3></header><div class="science-table-wrap"><table><thead><tr><th>Hypothesis</th><th>Intervention</th><th>Observed result</th><th>What it ruled out</th><th>Evidence</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><th scope="row">More coordinate bandwidth fixes credit</th><td>Coordinate-JVP Adam CIFAR control</td><td>Did not materially close the historical gap</td><td>Bandwidth alone was not enough; the control already used Adam-style m/v.</td><td>historical control</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">An exact sensor fixes C/S</th><td>Matched-JVP Control matched sensor substitution</td><td>Mixed / no consistent improvement across the two calibration seeds</td><td>Removing finite-difference coefficient error did not create missing directional coverage.</td><td>2-seed mechanism pilot</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Smoothing the learned prior fixes Learned Credit-Map Hybrid</th><td>Prior-smoothing development ablation</td><td>Did not pass its declared improvement gate</td><td>Temporal smoothing alone did not remove the residual quality gap.</td><td>archived development record</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Kernel fusion solves the remaining problem</th><td>Fused numerical variants</td><td>Some variants violated exactness checks</td><td>Systems optimization cannot trade away the mathematical contract.</td><td>numerical audit</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Exact grouped normalization is automatically faster</th><td>Exact grouped-normalization path</td><td>Mathematically exact path was slower</td><td>Correctness and speed were separate gates.</td><td>systems audit</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">MLP results transfer to convolution automatically</th><td>Historical CNN Probe Extension</td><td>42.264% vs 58.056% AdamW at 1,170 steps; 15.792 pp gap</td><td>The MLP result did not establish architecture transfer.</td><td>historical negative · 5 replicates</td></tr></tbody></table></div></section><aside id="claim-negative-results" class="claim claim-derived" data-claim-kind="derived"><header><span>Derived / analytical conclusion</span><code>claim-negative-results</code></header><div class="claim-body"><p>Negative width/depth, numerical, CNN, and systems results are retained in the main narrative because they constrain causal interpretations of the positive results.</p></div></aside></section>

###### Thread IV — a partial negative result, not a closed programme

<section id="thread-four" class="paper-section" data-section="08" aria-label="Thread IV — a partial negative result, not a closed programme"><aside class="in-brief" aria-label="Section summary"><span>In brief</span><div><p>The current evidence is <strong>semi-negative</strong>. Internally, Learned Credit-Map Hybrid retained an accuracy gap that increased with depth and incurred higher measured resident-step time than AdamW, while the 113.3M-parameter Two-Timescale Activation-Space ALOPEX result established execution rather than competitive convergence. Externally, PC-ALM raises the standard for local credit assignment with an exact linear-equilibrium result and deep residual-MLP image experiments. But no matched ALOPEX-versus-PC-ALM experiment was performed, and the PC-ALM paper and official reference implementation cited here do not establish Transformer or autoregressive language-model training.</p></div></aside><h3>Distinguish the problem classes</h3><p>Avoiding conventional backpropagation can be motivated by different constraints:</p><ul>
<li>layer-local or biologically motivated credit assignment;</li>
<li>activation-memory limits;</li>
<li>absence of derivative access;</li>
<li>nondifferentiable objectives;</li>
<li>delayed or discrete system outcomes;</li>
<li>black-box interfaces that expose only scalar evaluations.</li>
</ul><p>A method addressing one constraint is not automatically a comparator for the others. PC-ALM is relevant here because the ALOPEX program had moved into the <strong>local credit-assignment</strong> problem for ordinary differentiable feed-forward networks.</p><h3>Internal evidence at the point of closure</h3><p>The recorded Learned Credit-Map Hybrid results establish four facts relevant to that problem.</p><p>First, the canonical W512/d7 comparison gives Learned Credit-Map Hybrid about 57.79% accuracy and AdamW about 60.93%, a mean gap of approximately 3.13 percentage points across three paired seeds. Second, the gap increases in the recorded depth sweep: approximately 4.37 points at d16 and 5.16 points at d32. Third, the corrected resident-step timing snapshot records 5.203 ms for Learned Credit-Map Hybrid and 2.104 ms for AdamW, or approximately 2.47× the resident-step latency. Fourth, the 113.3M-parameter Two-Timescale Activation-Space ALOPEX result demonstrates mechanism execution at that parameter count; it is not a convergence or quality result.</p><p>These observations do not identify one cause for the quality gap. The depth microscope therefore keeps observed accuracy gaps separate from its non-empirical credit-retention model.</p><h3>What PC-ALM establishes</h3><p>PC-ALM is an augmented-Lagrangian predictive-coding method. It treats hidden activations as optimization variables and introduces a layer-local dual variable for each layer constraint. The local dynamics perform primal descent and dual ascent. In the linear setting, the paper proves that the equilibrium multipliers recover the backpropagation credit signals while communication remains local. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-seely2026pcalm" id="citation-seely2026pcalm-2">Seely &amp; Gould, 2026</a></span></p><p>For nonlinear residual MLPs, Seely and Gould report experiments through depth 128 and state that PC-ALM matches backpropagation performance across the tested width-depth regimes, including deep narrow networks in which standard predictive coding degrades. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-seely2026pcalm" id="citation-seely2026pcalm-3">Seely &amp; Gould, 2026</a></span> Their September 2026 Sakana AI publication extends the demonstration to residual MLPs through 1,000 layers on MNIST and reports performance within roughly two percentage points of backpropagation across that sweep. It also reports experiments on Fashion-MNIST, CIFAR-10, Tiny ImageNet, and ResNet-18 configurations. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-sakana2026pcalm1000" id="citation-sakana2026pcalm1000-1">Sakana AI, 2026</a></span></p><p>The theoretical and empirical claims have different scopes. Exact recovery of backpropagation credit is established for the linear equilibrium analysis. The nonlinear results are empirical. The 1,000-layer result is a later public extension rather than part of the May paper's depth-128 nonlinear study.</p><h3>Why PC-ALM raises the bar without closing the question</h3><p>The comparison is not a direct leaderboard because the implementations, architectures, tasks, and compute contracts differ. The decision follows from overlap in the scientific target.</p><div class="interactive-data compact" data-records="[{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;target&quot;,&quot;values&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;target&quot;,&quot;criterion&quot;:&quot;Target problem&quot;,&quot;alopex&quot;:&quot;Backprop-free/local hidden credit in differentiable networks&quot;,&quot;pcalm&quot;:&quot;Layer-local hidden credit in differentiable networks&quot;,&quot;interpretation&quot;:&quot;Direct problem overlap&quot;}},{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mechanism&quot;,&quot;values&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mechanism&quot;,&quot;criterion&quot;:&quot;Credit mechanism&quot;,&quot;alopex&quot;:&quot;Sparse directional measurement plus persistent or learned reconstruction&quot;,&quot;pcalm&quot;:&quot;Layer-local primal-dual inference&quot;,&quot;interpretation&quot;:&quot;Different mechanisms&quot;}},{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;exact&quot;,&quot;values&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;exact&quot;,&quot;criterion&quot;:&quot;Exact-credit result&quot;,&quot;alopex&quot;:&quot;Exact only along measured JVP directions; unmeasured span must be reconstructed&quot;,&quot;pcalm&quot;:&quot;Exact backpropagation credit at linear equilibrium&quot;,&quot;interpretation&quot;:&quot;Exactness is established over different objects; only PC-ALM provides full backprop-credit equivalence in its linear equilibrium analysis&quot;}},{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;depth&quot;,&quot;values&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;depth&quot;,&quot;criterion&quot;:&quot;Nonlinear depth&quot;,&quot;alopex&quot;:&quot;Recorded HFD-JP gap increases from d7 to d32&quot;,&quot;pcalm&quot;:&quot;Reported nonlinear residual-MLP experiments through d128&quot;,&quot;interpretation&quot;:&quot;PC-ALM depth range extends beyond the recorded HFD-JP sweep; protocols are not matched&quot;}},{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;extreme&quot;,&quot;values&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;extreme&quot;,&quot;criterion&quot;:&quot;Extreme depth / scale&quot;,&quot;alopex&quot;:&quot;FS-2T reaches 113.3M parameters by width/parameter count; no competitive deep result&quot;,&quot;pcalm&quot;:&quot;Public MNIST demonstration through d1000&quot;,&quot;interpretation&quot;:&quot;Parameter count and credit depth are separate scaling axes&quot;}},{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;systems&quot;,&quot;values&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;systems&quot;,&quot;criterion&quot;:&quot;Systems result&quot;,&quot;alopex&quot;:&quot;HFD-JP measured at ~2.47× AdamW resident-step latency in the corrected snapshot&quot;,&quot;pcalm&quot;:&quot;Iterative local inference; reported setup scales inference iterations with depth&quot;,&quot;interpretation&quot;:&quot;Neither record establishes a universal compute advantage&quot;}},{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;slm&quot;,&quot;values&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;slm&quot;,&quot;criterion&quot;:&quot;Transformer / SLM evidence&quot;,&quot;alopex&quot;:&quot;No autoregressive Transformer/SLM training result; the 113.3M record establishes execution only&quot;,&quot;pcalm&quot;:&quot;No Transformer/autoregressive language-model training result in the cited primary sources&quot;,&quot;interpretation&quot;:&quot;The SLM-scale question remains empirically open; image-MLP depth results cannot be treated as a matched language-model result&quot;}},{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;blackbox&quot;,&quot;values&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;blackbox&quot;,&quot;criterion&quot;:&quot;Black-box applicability&quot;,&quot;alopex&quot;:&quot;Derivative-free FS-2T can operate from function evaluations&quot;,&quot;pcalm&quot;:&quot;Requires differentiable local network dynamics&quot;,&quot;interpretation&quot;:&quot;PC-ALM does not address score-only black-box optimization&quot;}}]" data-columns="[{&quot;key&quot;:&quot;criterion&quot;,&quot;label&quot;:&quot;Criterion&quot;},{&quot;key&quot;:&quot;alopex&quot;,&quot;label&quot;:&quot;Recorded ALOPEX / learned-credit evidence&quot;},{&quot;key&quot;:&quot;pcalm&quot;,&quot;label&quot;:&quot;PC-ALM evidence&quot;},{&quot;key&quot;:&quot;interpretation&quot;,&quot;label&quot;:&quot;Methodological implication&quot;}]" data-filters="[]"><div class="data-toolbar"><label class="data-search"><span>Search</span><input placeholder="Filter rows…" value=""></label><span class="data-count">8 / 8 rows</span></div><div class="table-wrap data-table-wrap" role="region" tabindex="0" aria-label="ALOPEX/HFD and PC-ALM evidence compared only on explicitly named dimensions; this is not a matched leaderboard"><table class="interactive-data-table"><caption>ALOPEX/HFD and PC-ALM evidence compared only on explicitly named dimensions; this is not a matched leaderboard</caption><thead><tr><th scope="col"><button type="button" disabled="" class="sorted">Criterion<span aria-hidden="true"> ↑</span></button></th><th scope="col"><button type="button" disabled="" class="">Recorded ALOPEX / learned-credit evidence<span aria-hidden="true"> ↕</span></button></th><th scope="col"><button type="button" disabled="" class="">PC-ALM evidence<span aria-hidden="true"> ↕</span></button></th><th scope="col"><button type="button" disabled="" class="">Methodological implication<span aria-hidden="true"> ↕</span></button></th></tr></thead><tbody><tr data-row-id="blackbox" class=""><th scope="row">Black-box applicability</th><td>Derivative-free FS-2T can operate from function evaluations</td><td>Requires differentiable local network dynamics</td><td>PC-ALM does not address score-only black-box optimization</td></tr><tr data-row-id="mechanism" class=""><th scope="row">Credit mechanism</th><td>Sparse directional measurement plus persistent or learned reconstruction</td><td>Layer-local primal-dual inference</td><td>Different mechanisms</td></tr><tr data-row-id="exact" class=""><th scope="row">Exact-credit result</th><td>Exact only along measured JVP directions; unmeasured span must be reconstructed</td><td>Exact backpropagation credit at linear equilibrium</td><td>Exactness is established over different objects; only PC-ALM provides full backprop-credit equivalence in its linear equilibrium analysis</td></tr><tr data-row-id="extreme" class=""><th scope="row">Extreme depth / scale</th><td>FS-2T reaches 113.3M parameters by width/parameter count; no competitive deep result</td><td>Public MNIST demonstration through d1000</td><td>Parameter count and credit depth are separate scaling axes</td></tr><tr data-row-id="depth" class=""><th scope="row">Nonlinear depth</th><td>Recorded HFD-JP gap increases from d7 to d32</td><td>Reported nonlinear residual-MLP experiments through d128</td><td>PC-ALM depth range extends beyond the recorded HFD-JP sweep; protocols are not matched</td></tr><tr data-row-id="systems" class=""><th scope="row">Systems result</th><td>HFD-JP measured at ~2.47× AdamW resident-step latency in the corrected snapshot</td><td>Iterative local inference; reported setup scales inference iterations with depth</td><td>Neither record establishes a universal compute advantage</td></tr><tr data-row-id="target" class=""><th scope="row">Target problem</th><td>Backprop-free/local hidden credit in differentiable networks</td><td>Layer-local hidden credit in differentiable networks</td><td>Direct problem overlap</td></tr><tr data-row-id="slm" class=""><th scope="row">Transformer / SLM evidence</th><td>No autoregressive Transformer/SLM training result; the 113.3M record establishes execution only</td><td>No Transformer/autoregressive language-model training result in the cited primary sources</td><td>The SLM-scale question remains empirically open; image-MLP depth results cannot be treated as a matched language-model result</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div><p>The implication is specific. The repository contains (1) a Learned Credit-Map Hybrid quality gap that increased in the recorded depth sweep, (2) a corrected resident-step timing result above AdamW, and (3) no demonstrated quality result showing superiority over strong gradient baselines. PC-ALM addresses the same local-credit objective with an exact linear-equilibrium credit result and nonlinear residual-MLP experiments extending substantially beyond the recorded Learned Credit-Map Hybrid depth sweep. That invalidates any claim that ALOPEX is already the leading answer to local credit assignment; it does not establish how either method behaves on an autoregressive Transformer SLM.</p><p>On that evidence, the strongest claim must be withdrawn: the recorded experiments do <strong>not</strong> establish ALOPEX as a competitive general-purpose neural-network trainer. A stronger negative conclusion would require the experiment we do not yet have—a matched language-model study at SLM scale. PC-ALM is therefore a demanding external reference point, not the empirical result that closes the ALOPEX programme.</p><aside id="claim-partial-negative-neural" class="claim claim-derived" data-claim-kind="derived"><header><span>Derived / analytical conclusion</span><code>claim-partial-negative-neural</code></header><div class="claim-body"><p>Given the recorded Learned Credit-Map Hybrid depth/quality results and measured systems cost, the evidence rejects a claim of <strong>demonstrated general competitiveness</strong>. It does not reject the narrower hypothesis that an activation-space or learned-credit ALOPEX variant could remain viable at SLM scale; that claim has not yet been tested.</p></div></aside><p>The failure modes separate into measurement accuracy, credit coverage, state staleness, architecture transfer, and systems cost. These dimensions matter independently: terms such as “exact,” “local,” and “forward-only” do not determine training quality or computational efficiency.</p></section>

###### Thread V — the unresolved SLM frontier

<section id="thread-five" class="paper-section" data-section="09" aria-label="Thread V — the unresolved SLM frontier"><aside class="in-brief" aria-label="Section summary"><span>In brief</span><div><p>The current record is a <strong>partial negative result</strong>. It is negative for the strong claim that the tested ALOPEX descendants already rival mature gradient training on the recorded feed-forward image benchmarks. It is unresolved for a narrower question: whether activation-space or learned-credit ALOPEX can train an autoregressive small language model competitively enough to justify continued development when quality, compute, memory, and stability are measured together.</p></div></aside><h3>Parameter count was demonstrated; language-model training was not</h3><p>The 113.3M-parameter Two-Timescale Activation-Space ALOPEX experiment matters because it shows that the mechanism can be instantiated at a parameter count associated with small language models. It does <strong>not</strong> show that the method can train a language model, reach competitive validation loss, preserve long-horizon optimization stability, or do so at an acceptable compute budget.</p><p>That distinction is now central. Parameter scalability answers “can the state and update machinery exist at this size?” Training competitiveness asks a harder question: “does the method improve the model fast enough, accurately enough, and cheaply enough as the architecture and objective become language-model-like?”</p><p>The article therefore does not use the 113.3M result as an SLM result. It uses it as the upper endpoint of the current mechanism-scaling trajectory.</p><h3>PC-ALM is a stronger comparator, but not yet an SLM result</h3><p>PC-ALM materially changes the evidence landscape for local credit assignment. Its linear analysis connects equilibrium multipliers to exact backpropagation adjoints, and its nonlinear evidence extends through very deep residual MLPs on image tasks. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-seely2026pcalm" id="citation-seely2026pcalm-4">Seely &amp; Gould, 2026</a>, <a class="citation" href="#ref-sakana2026pcalm1000" id="citation-sakana2026pcalm1000-2">Sakana AI, 2026</a></span> That is stronger depth evidence than the current ALOPEX record.</p><p>But the scope is not interchangeable with the next ALOPEX question. The cited PC-ALM paper and official reference implementation do not report Transformer or autoregressive language-model training. The public 1,000-layer result is a residual-MLP MNIST experiment, not a 1,000-layer language model. This does not weaken the PC-ALM result; it limits what can be inferred from it.</p><p>Consequently, PC-ALM falsifies one narrative—“ALOPEX remains compelling simply because it avoids a conventional backward pass”—without settling another: whether the particular <strong>activation-space measurement + persistent state + actuator</strong> combinations developed here have useful scaling behavior in SLM training.</p><h3>Why an SLM is a different stress test</h3><p>A language model changes several interacting dimensions at once. Credit must move through repeated residual blocks, attention and feed-forward sublayers, normalization, embeddings and an autoregressive token loss. Sequence length changes activation-memory pressure. Weight sharing and repeated token statistics create structure that does not appear in the same form in the recorded CIFAR MLPs. The optimization target is evaluated over many token predictions rather than one image label.</p><p>None of those facts imply that ALOPEX will improve. They explain why an image-MLP comparison is not sufficient to assert failure or success at the language-model frontier.</p><p>The relevant hypotheses should therefore remain separated:</p><ol>
<li><strong>Quality hypothesis.</strong> The validation-loss/perplexity gap to AdamW may remain large or grow with scale.</li>
<li><strong>Scaling hypothesis.</strong> Activation-space intervention may avoid the parameter-dimensional degradation of naive parameter perturbation strongly enough that the gap behaves differently with width and depth.</li>
<li><strong>Systems hypothesis.</strong> A forward-heavy method may trade extra directional/JVP work for a different memory profile; that trade must be measured rather than inferred.</li>
<li><strong>Learned-credit hypothesis.</strong> A reusable credit operator may benefit from repeated Transformer structure, but its predictor bias, staleness, supervision cost and AdamW actuator remain confounds until ablated.</li>
</ol><aside id="claim-slm-open" class="claim claim-hypothesis" data-claim-kind="hypothesis"><header><span>Hypothesis / future test</span><code>claim-slm-open</code></header><div class="claim-body"><p>ALOPEX remains scientifically open only in this narrower sense: <strong>SLM-scale training is an untested regime, not a positive result</strong>. A language-model benchmark must earn any continuation of the neural-training programme.</p></div></aside><h3>What would count as evidence</h3><p>A credible SLM result cannot be “the code ran at 100M parameters.” It must report at least validation cross-entropy/perplexity against training tokens, wall-clock time, tokens per second, peak accelerator memory, forward/JVP/probe-equivalent work, failure rate across seeds, and the quality gap at matched budgets.</p><p>The same experiment should preserve the article's four-axis decomposition. A comparison that changes the sensor, state and actuator simultaneously can establish a complete-method result, but not which mechanism caused it. Therefore the full-method benchmark and the causal ablations must be reported separately.</p><p>The programme should also keep a strong null outcome. If the quality gap grows with model scale, if the method becomes substantially more expensive without a compensating memory advantage, or if long-horizon training becomes unstable, those are publishable negative results rather than reasons to change the benchmark after the fact.</p></section>

###### Predeclared SLM-scale experiment

<section id="experiment" class="paper-section" data-section="10" aria-label="Predeclared SLM-scale experiment"><aside class="in-brief" aria-label="Section summary"><span>In brief</span><div><p>Before moving the programme to agents, harnesses, or another optimization problem, the missing experiment is a matched language-model scale ladder. The purpose is not to prove ALOPEX can train an LLM. It is to determine whether the neural-training line deserves to remain open after the current MLP/CNN evidence.</p></div></aside><h3>Experimental question</h3><p>For a fixed decoder-only Transformer family and fixed data pipeline, how does the gap between ALOPEX-family credit assignment and ordinary backpropagation change as model size increases from a tiny language model to roughly the 100M-parameter regime?</p><p>The primary comparator is AdamW with ordinary reverse-mode backpropagation. The ALOPEX side should include at least the strongest strictly derivative-free activation-space branch and the strongest learned/forward-credit branch whose implementation is sufficiently frozen to reproduce. Methods that use JVPs or AdamW actuation must be labelled as such; they are not to be described as derivative-free.</p><p>PC-ALM should remain an external literature reference unless and until there is a controlled Transformer implementation under the same training protocol. An image-MLP result should not be inserted into the SLM table as though it were a matched baseline.</p><h3>Scale ladder</h3><p>The benchmark should use one architecture family and increase scale without changing the scientific question:</p><ul>
<li><strong>mechanism stage:</strong> approximately 1–5M parameters, short deterministic runs used to validate update ordering, loss accounting and instrumentation;</li>
<li><strong>small stage:</strong> approximately 10–30M parameters, enough training to expose optimization stability and early scaling behavior;</li>
<li><strong>SLM stage:</strong> approximately 80–150M parameters, where the existing 113.3M mechanism-execution result can be replaced by an actual language-model training result.</li>
</ul><p>Exact widths, depths, sequence length, vocabulary, data mixture and token budget must be frozen before the comparative runs. The model family should remain conventional enough that a strong AdamW baseline is straightforward and reproducible.</p><h3>Matching rules</h3><p>“Same number of optimizer steps” is not a sufficient budget. Each run should expose:</p><ul>
<li>training tokens consumed;</li>
<li>forward passes, backward passes, JVPs and perturbation probes;</li>
<li>forward-equivalent compute or measured accelerator time;</li>
<li>peak device memory and, if used, host/offload memory;</li>
<li>tokens per second;</li>
<li>validation cross-entropy and perplexity;</li>
<li>seed-to-seed variance and numerical failures.</li>
</ul><p>For the primary quality comparison, every method sees the same training data in the same order and the same validation split. For the systems comparison, report both matched-token and matched-wall-time views rather than selecting whichever makes one method look stronger.</p><h3>Causal ablations</h3><p>The complete methods answer whether a practical recipe works. The mechanism question requires controlled ablations:</p><ol>
<li>finite activation consequence versus matched activation JVP while holding state and actuator fixed;</li>
<li>stateless versus persistent C/S credit under the same measurement channel;</li>
<li>fixed/random low-rank basis versus refreshed basis at matched directional budget;</li>
<li>learned credit map versus non-learned persistent state under a common actuator where technically possible;</li>
<li>custom ALOPEX actuation versus Adam/AdamW actuation when the available evidence representation permits both.</li>
</ol><p>These ablations prevent a positive or negative full-method result from being attributed to the wrong component.</p><h3>Decision rule</h3><p>The article should not pre-write the outcome. Before running the scale ladder, freeze a competitiveness tolerance for validation loss/perplexity and a systems budget. Then interpret the result as follows:</p><ul>
<li><strong>gap narrows or stays bounded with a meaningful systems advantage:</strong> continue the neural-training programme;</li>
<li><strong>gap stays bounded but costs substantially more with no compensating constraint advantage:</strong> retain the scientific mechanism but not a competitiveness claim;</li>
<li><strong>gap grows materially with scale or training becomes unstable:</strong> strengthen the current partial negative result into a scale-dependent negative result;</li>
<li><strong>mixed outcome:</strong> report the regime boundary explicitly rather than collapsing it into “works” or “fails.”</li>
</ul><aside id="claim-slm-falsifier" class="claim claim-hypothesis" data-claim-kind="hypothesis"><header><span>Hypothesis / future test</span><code>claim-slm-falsifier</code></header><div class="claim-body"><p>The next decisive experiment is therefore <strong>ALOPEX on an actual autoregressive SLM under matched budgets</strong>, not a harness benchmark and not another parameter-count-only execution test.</p></div></aside></section>

###### Evidence, limitations, and publication contract

<section id="evidence" class="paper-section" data-section="11" aria-label="Evidence, limitations, and publication contract"><aside class="in-brief" aria-label="Section summary"><span>In brief</span><div><p>This article combines frozen experimental records, recomputed arithmetic, code-only historical audit, mathematical reasoning, external literature, and future hypotheses. It labels those evidence classes explicitly. The research sandboxes and browser simulations have been removed from the reader-facing retrospective; their source remains archived in the repository.</p></div></aside><p>The article labels frozen evidence, derived conclusions, limitations, literature, and prospective hypotheses at the point where each claim appears. This guards against a recurring failure in technical communication: a smooth narrative can make heterogeneous evidence sound equally certain.</p><p>The retrospective has several hard boundaries.</p><section class="science-panel threats-panel" aria-labelledby="threats-title"><header><span>Threats to validity</span><h3 id="threats-title">Three different uncertainty classes should not be collapsed into one disclaimer.</h3></header><div class="threats-grid"><article><strong>Internal / statistical</strong><ul><li>Only three paired seeds support the canonical and completed scaling quality summaries.</li><li>Paired initialization and batch hashes reduce known noise sources but do not eliminate tuning or implementation confounds.</li><li>Epochs within one trajectory and checkpoint-identical backend reruns are not independent replications.</li><li>The pilot is a sensor control inside C/S; comparisons with Learned Credit-Map Hybrid also change memory and actuation.</li><li>No multiplicity-adjusted inference is claimed across the scaling ladder.</li></ul></article><article><strong>External / construct</strong><ul><li>Positive quality evidence is limited to a residual-MLP CIFAR-10 family.</li><li>The historical CNN result belongs to another branch and is negative.</li><li>Autoregressive Transformers and SLM training remain untested by the neural-training evidence; LoRA, agents, verifier objectives and continual learning are separate untested regimes.</li><li>Equal optimizer steps are not equal objective-query, JVP, wall-time or memory budgets.</li><li>Resident-step latency is not end-to-end training throughput.</li></ul></article><article><strong>Source / review completeness</strong><ul><li>The MSc thesis is now a primary source; the exact submitted TensorFlow notebook remains missing, so recovered-code equivalence is unresolved.</li><li>Early HFD has a recovered digits mechanism source, but six requested historical artifacts remain unlocated.</li><li>A passing file hash verifies identity, not historical execution correctness.</li><li>The complete narrative verifier and checkpoint tensor comparison were not independently rerun during publication preparation.</li><li>Historical architecture and seed identities remain incomplete for some negative records.</li></ul></article></div></section><h3>Primary thesis located; submitted notebook still missing</h3><p>The MSc thesis is now available from the University of Guelph Atrium and can support page-level claims about the written work. <span class="citation-cluster"><a class="citation" href="#ref-roy2023thesis" id="citation-roy2023thesis-3">Roy, 2023</a></span> The exact submitted TensorFlow notebook remains unresolved, so equivalence between the recovered PyTorch translation and the thesis-era implementation must not be assumed.</p><h3>Partial early-HFD recovery</h3><p>The recovered digits mechanism source supports important update-order claims. It is not a complete reproduction of the historical HFD experimental family.</p><h3>Small seed counts</h3><p>Several headline comparisons rely on only three paired seeds. Exploratory intervals and paired initialization help interpretation but do not turn that sample into a strong equivalence test.</p><h3>Different compute contracts</h3><p>Equal optimizer steps do not imply equal sensor calls, tangent work, forward evaluations, resident-step time, or memory. Each systems claim must name its accounting unit.</p><h3>No demonstrated Transformer or SLM training result</h3><p>Nothing in the current evidence demonstrates ALOPEX on an autoregressive Transformer or small language model. The 113.3M-parameter record is mechanism execution, not language-model convergence. The cited PC-ALM evidence is also based on residual MLP/image-classification settings rather than a matched Transformer language-model protocol.</p><aside id="claim-publication-boundary" class="claim claim-limitation" data-claim-kind="limitation"><header><span>Limitation / evidence boundary</span><code>claim-publication-boundary</code></header><div class="claim-body"><p>The SLM-scale programme is a predeclared future test, not an extrapolated success claim. Agent, harness, verifier, user-feedback, and continual-learning optimization remain separate future questions and are not used as evidence for the neural-training result.</p></div></aside><h3>Reproducibility and provenance</h3><p>The publication evidence pipeline aims to retain the following provenance for empirical results:</p><ul>
<li>source artifact;</li>
<li>source hash;</li>
<li>method/cohort;</li>
<li>replication unit;</li>
<li>seeds;</li>
<li>split;</li>
<li>horizon;</li>
<li>calculation;</li>
<li>evidence class;</li>
<li>known limitations;</li>
<li>downloadable machine-readable values.</li>
</ul><p>A passing file hash verifies identity. It does not prove that the historical execution was correct. Recomputed arithmetic verifies the calculation from supplied rows. It is not an independent rerun of training.</p><p>Those distinctions are represented in the UI evidence classes and provenance panels. The separate schema-v2 campaign remains in staging. No new headline performance, causal or multiseed conclusion is valid until that campaign is complete and its release checks pass.</p><p>The article therefore treats provenance as an explicit publication requirement rather than an optional visual overlay. Claims remain qualified by the source and evaluation contract that supports them.</p></section>

###### Conclusion — a bounded negative result

<section id="atlas" class="paper-section" data-section="12" aria-label="Conclusion — a bounded negative result"><aside class="in-brief" aria-label="Section summary"><span>In brief</span><div><p>The current evidence does <strong>not</strong> establish ALOPEX as a competitive general-purpose replacement for backpropagation on the tested MLP/CNN workloads. This is a <strong>partial negative result</strong>: it constrains a demonstrated-competitiveness claim, not every possible ALOPEX variant or the untested autoregressive SLM regime.</p></div></aside><p>The strongest recorded learned-credit branch, Learned Credit-Map Hybrid, remained below AdamW in the canonical CIFAR comparison (approximately <strong>57.79% versus 60.93%</strong> across three paired seeds). Its accuracy gap widened with depth and exceeded the predeclared five-percentage-point practical threshold in the completed depth-32 cohorts. In the corrected resident-step timing snapshot, it took approximately <strong>2.47×</strong> as long as AdamW per step. The separate <strong>113.3M-parameter</strong> Two-Timescale Activation-Space execution demonstrates that its mechanism can run at large parameter count; it does not establish convergence, accuracy parity, or language-model training.</p><p>The central technical finding is the separation of <strong>measurement × credit space × persistent state × actuator</strong>. Making directional measurements exact did not solve missing directional coverage in the recorded matched pilot. Learning reusable credit improved the bounded mechanism but changed the model of persistent credit and the actuator, and did not remove the observed quality and systems limitations.</p><aside id="claim-final" class="claim claim-derived" data-claim-kind="derived"><header><span>Derived / analytical conclusion</span><code>claim-final</code></header><div class="claim-body"><p>The program establishes a useful account of the credit-assignment problem and its failure modes, <strong>not a competitive general neural-training optimizer</strong>. The negative evidence is specific to the recovered algorithms, benchmarks, seeds, depth regimes, and timing configurations.</p></div></aside><p>The main limitations are small seed counts, partially recovered historical sources, unequal access and compute contracts across some comparisons, and the absence of a matched autoregressive SLM training result. These limits prevent an inference of universal failure. A future SLM-scale comparison could narrow that uncertainty; it is <strong>not</strong> a result of this retrospective. The conclusion stands independently of that prospective experiment.</p><h3>Complete mathematical appendix</h3><p>The following reference retains <strong>all 38 equation entries</strong> from the former algorithm equation atlas, without live training, controls, or a simulated loss landscape. It covers the source-audited measurement and update contracts of six method families, including the AdamW comparison baseline. The derivations integrated with the main text remain in place; this appendix provides the complete method-by-method inventory.</p><section class="equation-atlas equation-atlas-static" aria-labelledby="equation-appendix-title"><header class="equation-atlas-head"><div><div class="figure-kicker">Mathematical appendix · complete method contracts</div><h3 id="equation-appendix-title">The equations behind each method</h3><p>Six algorithm families, with the original mathematical definitions and interpretation of every expression. Equations are method specifications, not evidence of training convergence or competitiveness.</p></div></header><section class="equation-panel" id="math-v43" aria-labelledby="math-v43-title"><header class="equation-summary"><span>Finite consequence · hidden function space</span><h4 id="math-v43-title">Activation-Space ALOPEX</h4><p>Activation-Space ALOPEX moves stochastic measurement from the full weight vector into hidden preactivation space, preserves per-example consequence, reconstructs local parameter evidence, and retains persistent normalized ALOPEX-family
